#2134. CorSera: Krösche already working on Milan’s January targets – two signings prioritised

AC Milan quietened some of the building criticism by beating Genoa before the break, and now planning for the future can continue.

According to what Corriere della Sera (via MilanNews) have written this morning, one thing is very clear from Milan’s victory against Genoa yesterday and their past few games in general: help is needed in the winter mercato.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The reason is simple: as long as the first-choice players are on the pitch everything is fine, but as soon as Ruben Amorim had to introduce a few second-string players, from that moment on Milan began to struggle and suffer more.

In the coming weeks, Markus Krösche will sign a contract and officially become the new Head of Football at Milan. In the meantime, he is already monitoring possible targets in view of an important window.

The priority remains a central defender who can come in and carry out Amorim’s instructions, which include building from the back. With Matteo Gabbia and Fikayo Tomori out injured for some time, the depth has been eroded drastically in this area, causing some to work overtime.

The paper adds that the feeling is a new centre-forward will arrive who can play alongside Gonçalo Ramos, given that Francesco Camarda is still deemed too young and for such a role. Then, a decision will be made on his fate too.

#2132. 3 players who Wrexham should offload in January, including Ryan Longman

Wrexham boss Phil Parkinson will be keen to add to his ranks, but inconsistent results have been a driving factor behind the Red Dragons’ lowly league position of 15th place.

The North Welsg have several players they could offload in January to free up funds to strengthen the squad, as they aim to close the six-point gap to QPR in the final play-off spot. A hefty outlay in the summer means Wrexham will need to be shrewd in the transfer market, with new arrivals likely requiring some departures to balance the squad.

Here, we take a look at three players Wrexham should look to offload in the winter to freshen up the squad and make way for new arrivals…

The experienced shot-stopper has become a forgotten figure in North Wales with just four appearances to his name since arriving in the summer of 2024.

Burton played second fiddle to Arthur Okonkwo in his debut campaign, before the latter and Danny Ward tussled for the No. 1 shirt last term. The £8m acquisition of Anthony Patterson in the summer has kept Burton as third choice, with his last appearance for the club coming over a year ago in a Carabao Cup defeat to Cardiff City.

Yet to feature in a matchday squad this season, the former Plymouth Argyle man may be enticed by the prospect of regular first-team football.

Ryan Longman has only had one Championship start this season and may seek a new challenge in January.  He asserted himself as a regular at right wing-back, making 21 League One appearances in the second half of the 2024/25 campaign as Wrexham secured promotion.

The 26-year-old was one of their most consistent performers when called upon last season too, deputising on both flanks, with his relentless work rate hailed by the Racecourse Ground faithful.

However, following the permanent acquisition of former loan favourite Issa Kabore from Manchester City in the summer and Danny Imray’s arrival, former Hull City ace Longman has been limited to substitute appearances. He is certainly capable of playing a more prominent role at another club.

Last but not least is Sebastian Revan, who has endured a torrid spell on the sidelines with injury. The defender could benefit from an EFL loan move upon his expected January comeback.

The Aston Villa academy graduate suffered a serious hamstring injury in February during his loan spell at Burton Albion, having made a positive impression under Gary Bowyer after playing a bit-part role in Wrexham’s promotion to the Championship.

His time at the Pirelli Stadium was cut short as he began a long-term rehabilitation programme with his parent club’s medical staff and is nearing a return to action.

Standing in the way of a starting spot is Jacques Ekomie, signed from Angers in the summer and in fine form. It means a loan or permanent exit for Revan looks increasingly likely.

#2131. Un talento al giorno, Djibril Ouziad: il terzino rivelazione del Le Havre

Djibril Ouziad, nato il 27 aprile 2009 a Le Havre, è un terzino destro franco-algerino del Le Havre. Cresciuto interamente nel settore giovanile del club normanno, ha firmato il primo contratto da professionista a settembre 2026 (fino al 2030) e ha esordito in Ligue 1 ad appena 17 anni. Nella stagione 2026/27 ha già collezionato una decina di presenze, spesso da titolare, dimostrando maturità e continuità nonostante le difficoltà della squadra. È anche nazionale francese Under 18.

Tecnicamente è un laterale con caratteristiche offensive di piede destro, abile nel cross, nel dribbling e nelle verticalizzazioni. Si distingue per dinamicità, capacità di spingere sulla fascia e predisposizione al tackle. Può ricoprire anche il ruolo di terzino sinistro. Le sue prestazioni precoci lo hanno messo nel mirino di club di Premier League come Liverpool e Aston Villa, che ne seguono con attenzione l’evoluzione.

#2130. Milan Futuro 3-0 Virtus CiseranoBergamo: Pandolfi shines in third straight win

It was a clean sweep for AC Milan’s men’s teams over the weekend, as Milan Futuro won their third straight in the league.

The Futuro got back in the win column last weekend as they managed to secure a thrilling 3-2 victory away against Virtus Verona on Sunday. Then, they followed that up by beating Piacenza 2-1 in midweek, in the game that had been suspended due to an injury to the referee.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

So, there was some good momentum for the second team heading into Sunday afternoon’s game, and they built on it with a commanding and convincing 3-0 home win over Virtus CiseranoBergamo.

Fabio Pandolfi opened the scoring just after the half-hour mark as he finished from close range after an excellent assist from Aurelien Guernier on the right, making it 1-0 which is how it would stay going into the break.

Early in the second half, Pandolfi got his second after a deflected left-footed shot wrong-footed the goalkeeper Rovelli and nestled into the net. In the closing stages, Cappelletti led a counter-attack and squared to Yahya Idrissi-Regragui, who made no mistake and made sure of the win.

It was another important result for Sergio Navarro’s side, who extended their unbeaten streak to six consecutive matches. Next up for the Rossoneri – who are third and two points off top – is another league game after the break against Tritium on 22 November at 14:30 CET.

#2129. Cisse discusses humble beginnings, choosing Milan, Italy call-up, Amorim and amazing form

Alphadjo Cisse has shed some light on his journey to becoming an AC Milan player, as well as what his first few months at the club have been like.

It has been a simply remarkable rise for Cisse, who at the start of 2026 was playing in the second division with Catanzaro, on loan from Hellas Verona. Milan bought him from there but left him with his loan club, before a serious injury needed surgery and ruled him out for the rest of the season.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Fast-forwarding to early November, and the 20-year-old is currently the top scorer for the Rossoneri with eight goals, the most recent of which came in Sunday’s 2-1 win over Genoa. There is talk of an imminent renewal already, while Roberto Mancini gave him a senior Italy call-up.

Cisse gave an exclusive interview to Cronache di Spogliatoio in which he touched upon various topics from his upbringing to the brilliant moment he is currently experiencing. MilanNews relayed his words.

“My life changed in my father’s factory. A Verona manager went to meet him at work to beg him to take me to their place. He’d been a worker forever: every day he’d leave work at 5:00pm, and by 5:15 he was already outside our house, calling me and taking me to play soccer.

“I still remember the first time. It was afternoon. He came up to the house and said, ‘Come on, I’ll take you somewhere’. And we went to the pitch. He was the one who instilled in me the passion for soccer, and from that moment on, I’ve never stopped.

“My parents made so many sacrifices to support me on this journey: my mom, when she had days off from work, would come up with me to take me on the bus to practice.”

“My mom is a very demanding mom. But she helps me stay focused and prevent fame from overwhelming me. So everything becomes easier with her. They tell me every day that they’re proud of me, but they also do everything they can to keep me grounded.

“Carlos, Milan’s assistant coach, often tells me this too: ‘ Surround yourself with people who truly love you and who help you stay focused’. I’m sure of this: I carry with me the carefree attitude of the child I was. I play with a smile. It’s hard not to have that in these first few months.”

You have just been called up by the Italian national team for the first time…

“I was on my way to do the pre-match interview against Ferencváros in the Europa League and they stopped me to say, ‘Alpha, they’ve called you up to the national team… the senior team!’ I had the match, I only realised afterwards.

“I want to prove I can compete. The call was in the air, they’d been talking to me about it for days… but this is my big chance. I haven’t had the chance to stop and think about my career. Football moves fast.”

“A few weeks ago, they asked me how much of my potential as a player I’d shown. I said 70%. Can I say that? Maybe I need to lower it a bit… let’s say 40%, because I have so many aspects to improve on, but at the same time, I know who I am and what I’m worth.

“Last season, Aquilani – who was my coach at Catanzaro – told me that ‘if you don’t play at least 10 years in Serie A, I think you’ll have failed’. I took it as a positive motivation, a phrase that pushed me to always do better and believe in what I can do.

“One of my friends in football is Favasuli, I met him just last year in Serie B. We often found ourselves talking… daydreaming. I still remember the day he said to me: ‘Think about when one day we’ll face two great teams’.

“I smiled. ‘It would be great to meet again in Serie A, but maybe it’s a little too late for me, I’m already 23…’ Instead, he made it too: Milan-Napoli will be in December, we’ll see. He was my driver, he drove me to camp. He’s proof that there’s no age to stop feeling young or believing in it, no matter the path.”

Your path has already taken you from Verona to Catanzaro and to Milan…

“In those very months, my life changed again. No longer in my father’s factory. In a matter of days, I had gone from wondering how far I was from Serie A to finding myself facing the most important decision of my career.

“I’ll be honest: I didn’t take that moment very well. When I arrived at Catanzaro, I had only played two five-minute games in Serie A, including against AC Milan. Aquilani and manager Polito really wanted me, they pushed me. It was my first experience away from home.

“In just a few months, I scored a lot, everyone wanted me. And in January, I was practically on a flight to Holland, bound for Rotterdam: to sign with PSV. But then AC Milan came along.”

“My friends’ WhatsApp group exploded: ‘Alpha, is it true?!’ And I was like, ‘Yes guys, yes!’ I didn’t want to make the wrong choice: PSV wanted me right away, while Milan would have let me finish the season in Serie B.

“Two great teams, but Milan… how do you explain that? I was experiencing a mix of emotions; my parents told me to stay calm and that the best was yet to come. We decided this was the right choice. And judging by how things are going, I’d say we were right.”

“The first few times I came to Milanello, I did so to recover from my injury. Milan left me at Catanzaro, yes, but after a few days I got injured. In fact, I had serious doubts: ‘I’m coming to AC Milan after an injury, let’s see how it goes’.

“This is exactly what Amorim spoke to me about as soon as we met. We focused on the injury, and he explained what he expected of me on the pitch. He’s a great guy, and he knows how to deal with us young players. And he knows how to keep me grounded. Even though I’m not someone who likes to let my mind wander.”

You kept a promise after your Europa League hat-trick…

“Even after my hat-trick in the Europa League, I have to say that everything was already clear to me: it was simply confirmation to myself that I can compete at this level. I didn’t see it as a point of arrival, but as a promise kept to the child I was.

“And above all, as a promise to keep. Even though I was coming back from a long injury, even though I’m young and have so many areas to improve, I never had any doubts about my abilities or where I could go. I’m very demanding of myself, just like my mother.”

“Modrić gave me some advice on what I need to improve: I need to read the game better and understand the moments of the match. He’s a great player, a spectacular person, willing to help you learn, and interesting to listen to.

“Even though my idol was very different as a kid: Paul Pogba, when he was at Juventus. We do different things on the pitch, and his haircut certainly didn’t go unnoticed by a kid… maybe I’ll get one in the future! What I appreciated about him was that he made people laugh: that’s what I want to do too.

“I was inspired by the player whose jersey I had as a child: Clarence Seedorf. My mother gave it to me; it was the one I wore on the pitch. So maybe it was just my destiny to come here. Seedorf became an idol at San Siro.

“The place where you feel the grandeur, the pressure, the feeling of playing in a stadium that represents a childhood dream. Being there now is like saying to the child I was: ‘See? You did it’. It’s a huge motivation for me. But I’m carefree enough not to be affected by it.

“When you start playing, you notice it less and you don’t pay attention: it’s easier. The fans are incredibly attached to the team. You notice it not just in Milan, but all over the world. And that’s how you understand where you are. I was really impressed by the organisation here.”

Do you like to play with a certain freedom?

“I’m very carefree when I go on the pitch. Whether it’s playing in Serie B or going to San Siro, I think it’s normal for me. I’m the same person I always am. As a kid, I was obsessed with bicycle kicks. I scored five or six incredible goals… now I avoid them!

“In the Verona youth team, I had a coach, Paolo Sammarco, who shaped the way I play today: he gave me a lot of freedom, helping me understand how I should express myself.”

Is it important for young Italian players to have confidence?

“Italy is full of strong young players, I assure you. It all depends on the opportunities you’re given. Many have emerged in recent months, including me, and I’m sure many more will emerge in the near future.

“When you reach a certain level, you’re amazed: one above all, I was struck by Pisilli, he’s simply amazing. I knew him, but when I played with him, I understood his strength. And I understood my own strength when, in my early years in Verona, I approached the big boys.

“The level was rising, but I continued to have faith, to understand that I could achieve great things if I followed the path I had. That I had something special.”

Your life has changed a lot, are you still the same person?

“My life has changed so much in the past few months, perhaps more than I can even realize. There are moments when I stop and think about how different everything is from just a short while ago. Football is taking me to places I dreamed of, but inside I’m still the same boy.

“But I know what my ambitions are. And perhaps that’s exactly what helps me not lose myself, even when everything changes. I try to enjoy every moment, without forgetting where I started from and all the people who have accompanied me to this point.”

Finally, clear something up: how do you say your name?

“My name is Cisse, without an accent. My name can be read any way, but it’s pronounced ‘Sisse’. I even had to send a voice message to the commentators!”

#2128. One league appearance despite trio of assists: How is former Burnley and West Ham target faring since failed summer move

Burnley were a busy side at the end of the transfer window as they looked to strengthen following a disappointing summer and start to the window. The Clarets came close to signing Sunderland winger Jocelin Ta Bi on deadline day in September, but the move ultimately collapsed over the Clarets’ squad requirements.

The 21-year-old had travelled to Turf Moor as Nicky Hayen’s side explored a loan deal, only for the transfer to fall through late in the window. Burnley were not alone in their pursuit, with West Ham, Hull City and several French clubs also expressing interest.

According to reports from Sebastian Denis, the Hammers were among those monitoring Ta Bi throughout the summer, underlining the competition for his signature.

The Ivory Coast youngster joined Sunderland from Maccabi Netanya in January 2026 on a four-and-a-half-year contract. However, regular first-team opportunities have been difficult to come by under Regis Le Bris.

Ta Bi has made limited Premier League appearances, totalling fewer than 60 minutes across his senior outings in England’s top flight. His opportunities have largely come in cup competitions, where he has shown encouraging signs of his potential.

His standout performance came against Brentford in the Carabao Cup on 28 October. Starting on the right wing, Ta Bi was heavily involved in all three Sunderland goals during their 3-2 victory, creating several opportunities and showcasing his ability to beat opponents in wide areas.

Le Bris was particularly impressed by his technical quality, bravery and work rate, highlighting how the youngster managed to combine his attacking contributions with considerable defensive effort.

The Sunderland head coach has previously spoken positively about Ta Bi’s potential, although regular minutes remain important for his development. A temporary move could therefore still make sense, particularly given the player’s limited Premier League involvement.

That is something Burnley should continue monitoring. Ta Bi possesses explosive pace, close control and the dribbling ability to trouble Championship defenders. His preference for cutting inside from the right onto his stronger left foot could also offer a different attacking dimension.

Those qualities would have suited Burnley’s need for greater pace and penetration in the final third under Hayen. Although the Belgian has since departed, the Clarets’ need for more attacking threat has not disappeared.

Ta Bi still has plenty to prove at senior level, but his performance against Brentford demonstrated why Sunderland rate him so highly. If first-team opportunities remain limited, Burnley could have another opportunity to revisit their interest in a player they came so close to signing.

#2127. Cisse like the greats, Amorim surpasses Allegri: All the key stats from Genoa 1-2 AC Milan

AC Milan won their seventh Serie A game of the season on Sunday afternoon, beating a Genoa side that entered the game in great form.

The home crowd at the Marassi were fired up going into this one given Genoa had won four of their last five. However, goals from Alphadjo Cisse and Christian Pulisic inside the opening 20 minutes was exactly what Milan needed to take the ascendancy.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Ellertsson did score inside the final few minutes as Genoa mounted a late surge in the hopes of snatching a point, but the Rossoneri held on to win 2-1 and move back into the top four places in the table.

Looking at the general stats makes for interesting reading. Milan dominated the ball with 62% possession, but Genoa had nearly double the Expected Goals (1.14 to 0.61), even though big chances ended up being even at two apiece.

The Rossoblu also had nearly triple the shots (17 to 6) and shots on target (5 to 2), while the Rossoneri did not win a single corner. It suggests that Ruben Amorim’s side simply took their moments and sat back, though maybe Daniele De Rossi can feel a bit hard done by.

📊 The stats from the win against Genoa. pic.twitter.com/wr1E6cohcZ

— MilanData📊 (@acmilandata) November 8, 2026

Alphadjo Cisse is one of only three players born in 2006 or later to have scored 8+ goals across all competitions in Europe’s top five leagues. The others are Lamine Yamal (13) and Paris Brunner (8).

In terms of minutes per goal in Serie A, Cisse leads the way with one every 81 minutes. That puts him ahead of the likes of Donyell Malen (one every 82), Lautaro Martinez (one every 88) and Gustavo Varela (one every 91).

After 13 appearances for Milan, Cisse is level with Filippo Inzaghi, Andriy Shevchenko and Alberto Gilardino on 8 goals. Pierino Prati (10) and Mario Balotelli (12) are the only players to record more.

Milan are unbeaten in their last 11 Serie A matches against Genoa (W7, D4). Since 2020-21, the Rossoneri only have a longer run of unbeaten games against Hellas Verona (12).

The Diavolo have won each of their last five Serie A away games against Genoa. Only against one team do the Rossoneri currently have a longer running away win streak in the competition: Hellas Verona (seven).

Milan have won three and drawn three of their first six away games this season. The Rossoneri have pnly avoided defeat in their first six away games of the season on three occasions between 2020-21 and 2025-26, the same number as Inter and Napoli, and could now become the only team to do so four times in this span, including the current season.

With this victory, AC Milan climbed to 24 points in the standings, two more than the number earned by Massimiliano Allegri’s Milan last season after 11 games. This is also better than Stefano Pioli’s last season, also by two points.

#2126. Bristol City & West Ham ticketing twist has caused a huge backlash

West Ham have enforced a major ticketing decision involving away supporters ahead of next month's home clash against Bristol City - and it's causing quite the reaction online.

Following West Ham's unprecedented relegation from the Premier League back in May, the London Stadium represents the away day for away fans throughout the Championship.

Indeed, the second-tier has never seen a larger ground than the London Stadium's 60,000-capacity, and for virtually every fanbase, an away trip to this corner of East London is one of the most notable fixtures on the calendar all season.

Fans of Bristol City, however, have just received a fresh twist that will impact their fixture away to West Ham next month, with the decision stirring up mixed reaction among supporters online.

Bristol City fans will be ticking off a major Championship away day when they travel to West Ham on December 12, though trips to Middlesbrough, West Bromwich Albion and Derby County are all on the agenda before their journey down the M4 and into East London.

But it has come with a twist that will now see a reduced number of supporters in attendance within the London Stadium away end, with West Ham having reciprocated the Robins' travelling allocation.

A ticketing announcement from the West Country outfit has confirmed this, disclosing that tickets will be on sale to supporters from next Wednesday before revealing the allocation.

Now, the Robins have informed fans that they have received an allocation of just 2,110 tickets including rail seats and general seating. This, of course, is a fairly sizeable reduction on the Hammers' typical away allocation to travelling supporters of around 3,000, yet they've simply reciprocated what City have already done.

Football League World assesses and ranks the FIFTEEN best away fanbases in the EFL right now

The development comes following an announcement in September which revealed that Bristol City have opened the Atyeo Stand to home fans for high-profile fixtures against West Ham, Stoke and Severnside rivals Cardiff, thus reducing the number of tickes available to away supporters.

Therefore, West Ham have reacted by reducing their own allocation for the Robins faithful, and unsurprisingly, it's sparked something of a stir.

With every other Championship club to have travelled to West Ham so far this season having received a larger allocation, fans were quick to react following the Robins' announcement.

A lot of the reaction actually came from Hammers supporters, who wasted no time in lauding their club's reciprocal efforts.

This one fan, for example, applauded his side and urged them to do the same for every game against an opposing club that doesn't provide the same allocation.

Another, meanwhile, kept it direct and straight-to-the-point in response to a Bristol City punter who had questioned the allocation.

Indeed, this West Ham supporter believes the decision is evidence of the East Londoners actually getting something right, with many simply all-too-happy to see that their club has refused to simply roll over and give out the largest possible allocations while being restricted in some of their own travels on the road.

By contrast, Bristol City fans have naturally failed to share the same sentiment and believe that the Robins have been hard done by with this particular decision.

A Robins supporter here pulled no punches by describing it as an "absolute joke" - to which the Hammers' lot were, of course, swift in reminding of why the allocation has been shaved.

Emojis can sometimes tell the story, too, and in this case, they summed up exactly how another supporter of the Ashton Gate side is feeling right now.

However, another punter saw sense behind West Ham's decision and has backed the reciprocation.

There has been a split of opinion over West Ham's allocation for the travelling Bristol City contingent next month, even if many do believe that the Hammers are perfectly within their right to match the reduction.

However, there's absolutely no division when it comes to the prices that West Ham are charging for Robins fans who intend on making the trip to the London Stadium, which may just work out among their cheapest away days all season.

Adult tickets come cheaper than virtually anywhere else in the Championship at just £25, whereas 66+ and under-21 ones are even more affordable at a price of £12.

Under-18 tickets, meanwhile, will cost no more than £10, with these prices across the board paling in comparison to some other second-tier clubs.

West Ham and Bristol City fans, what do you make of this ticketing decision? Is it fair? Let us know your thoughts in the comments below!

All in all, then, Bristol City supporters perhaps can't complain much, and in terms of how much the fixture will cost to attend, there can be no faulting the Hammers.

That's why this fan, despite the noise, took time to praise the prices for City's trip to face Scott Parker's side away from home.

It's also no shock that one West Ham supporter - and many more silently, one can only imagine - hopes that Bristol City undertake some reciprocation of their own by charging the same prices when the promotion-chasers travel to Ashton Gate on New Year's Day.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2125. Billy Sharp sends EFL Championship promotion warning to Birmingham City

EFL legend and all-time Championship top scorer Billy Sharp has warned Birmingham City that there's still "a long way to go" after Chris Davies' side reached the second-tier summit ahead of the ongoing international break.

The Blues enjoyed an exceptional run of games between the three-week break in September and October and this current one, winning six of their seven games, three by at least a three-goal margin.

They dropped points only at Cardiff City, a side that has yet to lose at home this season, and have maintained their own unbeaten home record, winning all four games to add some gloss to a St Andrew's record which was pretty draw-heavy in the infancy of the campaign.

The win against former table-toppers Swansea City saw them climb above Middlesbrough on goal difference, and they will hold that top spot for at least another week until action resumes ahead of the Christmas period.

The question in the meantime, though, is: Can they stay there?

Billy Sharp has been a regular pundit on EFL shows this season, after formally announcing his retirement from the professional game back in October. The 40-year-old was promoted out of the Championship three times in his career, so he knows what it takes to maintain top-two form.

The all-time Championship top scorer waxed lyrical over Birmingham through the opening 15 games of the campaign, and was actually surprised that their run to the top of the table hasn't been given as much attention as it arguably should have.

FLW looks at which of the division's 24 clubs are the biggest and lowest spenders

"I think you saw glimpses last season, but they obviously didn't have that consistency," he told the Essential EFL Podcast. "They've done it really quietly, actually; no one's really spoken about them.

"He’s got the two settled centre-halves, which I've liked since last season, and I think he's playing a 4-2-3-1, which really suits him at the minute.

"He seems to have a settled side and had a little sprinkle with Millar coming in, adding a bit of magic down the left, and Stansfield got the two goals, which should do him a world of good. We know he’s got the ability, and when he’s firing, he can win games for Birmingham."

It's so far, so good for the Blues this season, but we are just 15 games in. With over two-thirds of the year still to go, and just one point separating Chris Davies' side from West Brom in fifth, Sharp feels that they can't rest on their laurels at all.

"Am I surprised they're top? I am," he admitted. "But we were saying this about Swansea on the last international break.

"It’s still tight at the top, fair play to Birmingham; going into this international break they are top, but there’s still a long way to go."

As Sharp mentioned, Birmingham's current run of six wins in seven to reach the top of the Championship has gone slightly under the radar, with the main second-tier narratives surrounding West Ham United's drop-off, or Middlesbrough struggling without Will Lankshear, for example.

Now, though, this Blues side are at the forefront of the conversation, and will be until league action resumes after the international break. Chris Davies' side are there to be shot at, and all eyes will be on whether they can handle the weight of expectation.

It's arguable that they didn't last season, with many expecting them to follow in the footsteps of Ipswich Town and make it back-to-back promotions after their record-breaking 111-point campaign in League One the year prior.

Pick any two players. Send the code, and your mate enters it here to play the same pair.

But this year, the squad is much more settled. Partnerships which were forming last year are fixed now, and there hasn't been as much squad churn as there was last summer at St Andrew's.

Plus, Chris Davies' decision to move away from a patient, heavy possession-based style has worked wonders, with the Blues packing a real punch in an attacking sense due to their desire to get the ball forward quicker.

They look a lot more ready to maintain results and handle pressure as they become the side teams want to knock off the pedestal.

Sharp also spoke about how this exact conversation was being made around Swansea's chances at promotion, with Vitor Matos' side sitting atop the Championship at the last international break.

And while the Swans did return to action with back-to-back wins, they followed that up with four defeats in five leading up to the break, including that loss at St Andrew's last time out.

They're still in contention, sitting three points off Birmingham, but the difference in performances with the pressure on, compared to when it's not, was there to be seen, and Blues have to guard against the same happening to them.

Do you think Birmingham City can maintain this promotion-winning form? Let us know in the comments.

Chris Davies' side have a tough matchup at Stoke City when play resumes, which then leads them into a run of four games against sides sitting between 18th and 22nd. A win at the bet365 Stadium would propel them into a run of, on paper, easier matches.

Perhaps, by mid-December, this could be a side with some daylight between themselves and the chasing pack, and then people may be taking their current title charge a lot more seriously.

#2124. Sky: Ramos involved but has minimal support – the numbers that illustrate Amorim’s point

Some interesting analysis on AC Milan and Goncalo Ramos’ attacking struggles has been provided by the Sky studios.

It was a familiar tale for Ramos during Milan’s 2-1 win over Genoa on Sunday. He was involved but rarely inside the box, then he was selfless instead of ruthless in front of goal when he chose to square for Christian Pulisic to make it 2-0.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The worries are starting to grow regarding whether the Portuguese can provide the output required of a number nine that cost €70m. Yet, the Rossoneri have scored 23 goals in 11 league games which suggests goals are coming from elsewhere, sometimes with Ramos’ help.

Sky offered some interesting insight after Sunday’s game. Firstly, they picked out just how involved Ramos actually is with various aspects of Milan’s overall play.

For example, he is first among Serie A strikers for interceptions and recoveries, while he is second for receptions, touches and duels. He is third for passes.

He is first among centre-forwards in the league for touches in the middle third of the field and touches in the opposition half. He is second for fouls committed plus fouls suffered among forwards, with 41.

Sky then shift the focus to the team’s overall play to try and explain why Ramos perhaps isn’t getting the quality service and is having to drop deeper as a result.

They look at data per 30 minutes of possession. Milan are first in Serie A for build-up actions, but they are 20th for entries into the final third and entries into the opposition box.

Amorim’s side are 19th for percentage of touches in the attacking third and 18th in percentage of touches in the opposition box. With numbers like these, there isn’t much for Ramos to get on the end of.

What does this tell us? Well, Amorim’s post match assessment about struggling to get the ball from the defensive third into the final third are true. Milan have a lot of the ball but still struggle to progress it, as the coach told Sky.

“I think it’s difficult for a striker when he doesn’t score goals. But I’ll repeat: we don’t spend much time in the final third of the pitch. We have possession, we’re improving our ball output, but we’re missing that. When we’re consistently in the final third, Gonçalo will score a lot of goals,” he said.

“It’s a team problem, we’re not helping Ramos, but he’s helping us a lot. It’s a process, a journey. And we’re not yet at the point of being so dominant that Ramos spends most of his time in the opponent’s penalty area. We’re still a long way off.”

#2123. Bradford City TV decision announced and it's causing a reaction

Bradford City fans have been left extremely frustrated by a television broadcast decision impacting their FA Cup tie against non-league Wimborne Town next month.

The Bantams have endured a dramatic opening to the new League One season, with Michael Duff recently replacing Graham Alexander as the man in the Valley Parade hotseat.

Duff has only overseen one league victory in West Yorkshire so far, but his new employers are certainly going well in cup competitions, yet to lose in the EFL Trophy whilst also making the EFL Cup quarter-finals and reaching the FA Cup second round.

Bradford comfortably dispatched Marine 7-0 on Saturday afternoon, with said victory being rewarded with a high-profile tie against Wimborne, the lowest-ranked side now left in the competition, after the Dorset club defeated League Two side Crawley Town 2-0 on Sunday.

There are, of course, plenty of former Premier League sides competing in the FA Cup's early rounds, and the likelihood of them competing against non-league sides increases the chances of television coverage.

Eight ties were selected for broadcast in the first round proper, and the same number have been chosen again for the second round between December 4th and 7th.

All 24 League One clubs have been ranked in order of size.

Bradford are one of six League One sides who will see their ties televised, with their trip to the Wyatt Homes Stadium kicking off the weekend on Friday, December 4th, with coverage beginning on TNT Sports 1 at 7PM ahead of a 7:30PM kick-off.

Other televised ties include 2021 FA Cup winners, Leicester City's trip to Dover Athletic, which now kicks off at 5:15PM on Saturday, December 5th on BBC Two.

It is approximately five hours' worth of travel from the West Yorkshire city to the town of Wimborne Minster, meaning that, at the best of times, an arduous journey would be in store for the Bradford fans.

Crawley supporters received an allocation of 500 tickets in the 3,250-capacity ground last weekend, and a similar number will be expected to make the trip to support Duff's men.

However, whilst City fans are likely to sell out such a figure, the decision to now play the game on a Friday night just three weeks before Christmas hasn't gone down well at all, with one supporter saying: "Asking people to take a day off work to go do a 10 hour round trip to Winbourne is crazy. Deserves boosted loyalty points imo."

Another said of the decision: "Friday night on the south coast. Seeing as there will only be about 200 tickets it should reduce demand."

"Did we really expect anything else - well actually the way the schedulers don't give a damn about travel times I had a feeling it might have been Monday!," one displeased City fan wrote.

"Who makes these decisions," questioned another Bantams supporter.

Bradford fans, will you be making the long journey to Wimborne for this FA Cup tie? Comment below!

Another verdict read: "Teed up perfectly for #bcafc to be on the receiving end of an FA Cup shock. Friday night, tight ground, locals right up for it, live tv....all the ingredients right there!"

"Honestly mind boggling who makes these decisions. Fair play to anyone who makes that journey on a Friday night," backed up the general feeling amongst Bradford's fanbase.

Wimborne are currently ninth in the Southern Football League Premier Division South, with four steps in the English pyramid separating themselves from Bradford.

As such, the wider public will certainly be hoping the non-league side reach the third round and potentially pluck out a 'glamour tie' involving a Premier League or high-flying Championship outfit.

Each day there are five current or former EFL players to identify. For each one you’ll see their senior clubs in the order they played for them. Loan spells are left out.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

Notts County's League One struggles look to be over after their four matches in a row lost in September and October, with Martin Paterson finally finding the winning formula again.

Paterson guided the Magpies back into League One earlier this year with a resounding League Two play-off final victory over Salford City, and he was backed by the Reedtz Brothers with some exciting cash signings and eye-catching loanees.

Football League World runs down their Team of the Season in League One so far this term

County were in the relegation zone of the third tier in mid-October after four defeats on the spin, but wins over Sheffield Wednesday and Mansfield Town, as well as a respectable draw at home to Stockport County, has seen the mood lift at Meadow Lane, and the pressure somewhat rise from Paterson's shoulders.

Last weekend, Notts County stepped away from league action to take part in Round One of the FA Cup, where they were handed a potential banana skin in the form of National League side Altrincham.

In-front of over 4,000 fans at Moss Lane, County went 2-1 behind to the fifth tier outfit, only for Cal Roberts to rescue the game in the 84th-minute and send it to extra time.

And despite the deteriorating foggy conditions, Notts County won out in the resulting penalty shootout, despite the lack of visibility for their supporters at the opposite end of the ground.

One of 40 clubs that were in Monday's second round draw, Notts were handed a tie against a club even further down the footballing pyramid than their previous opponents, with a trip to Northern Premier League side Guiseley on the horizon.

The West Yorkshire outfit, based around nine miles north of the city of Leeds, made it through to round two by beating Sutton United from two divisions above 1-0, and they have now been rewarded with another home clash with a much bigger outfit.

They got over 1,500 fans through the gates of Nethermoor Park last weekend, and that may be even bigger with the visit of a League One outfit, which was scheduled to take place on the weekend of December 5.

#2120. 6 EFL clubs who must eye £0 Wilfried Zaha transfer

Championship clubs will already have one eye on the January transfer window as they look to strengthen their squads, even if there is a lot of football to be played between now and the New Year.

Another problem facing clubs trying to do deals in January is the reluctance of rivals to sell players halfway through the season.

It’s obvious why that is the case, so deals in the winter window often come at a premium, which makes business harder to do.

However, one market clubs could explore is the free agent one, as there are still some talented individuals who are without a club.

Wilfried Zaha certainly falls into that category, with the Crystal Palace legend yet to return to the game since his Galatasaray contract expired in the summer.

The forward last played on loan at Charlotte FC in May, so he would take time to get up to speed, but, even at 34, you still get the feeling Zaha could contribute a lot.

Football League World looks at the average attendance table in the Championship so far this season

The Ivory Coast international had been linked with a move to Lazio last month, but nothing materialised, and, given his age, he may want to get back playing in the UK, where he has spent most of his life.

With that in mind, we look at SIX EFL clubs who should consider a move for Zaha…

Wrexham have signed several big-names in their journey from non-league to the Championship, and Zaha could help them take that next step to the Premier League.

His profile would make this an intriguing signing, but, more importantly, he would beef up Phil Parkinson’s squad, which is lacking in the final third. The likes of Josh Windass, Nathan Broadhead and Sam Smith have suffered injuries, with the latter ruled out for the season.

So, in January, Wrexham are widely expected to bring in at least one attacker, but they could still benefit from Zaha as well, who could play in the three attacking roles in Parkinson’s XI, and he would provide the team with some much-needed pace and spark.

Birmingham are another ambitious club at this level, and they will feel that promotion to the Premier League is a very real possibility considering they sit top of the league right now.

But, the one concern for Chris Davies will centre on the squad depth, as the lack of quality behind their best XI is a worry. So, adding a player like Zaha would make a lot of sense, as he could provide quality cover down both flanks.

Given his pedigree, Zaha would back himself to force his way into the best XI, and his experience and know-how could be just what Blues need to get over the line this season.

As mentioned, Zaha has spent most of his life in London, so, he would surely be open to playing in the capital once again, and West Ham would be a good fit.

After a positive start, Manor Solomon’s form has dipped, and with Edvin Austbo and Jarrod Bowen also at the club, you could argue the Hammers are one winger short, so Zaha could add to the squad for this season at least.

Where do West Ham need to strengthen in January? Let us know in the comments below.

This campaign is all about promotion for West Ham, so a short-term solution can’t be dismissed, and if Zaha helps the Irons back to the top flight, it would be the perfect outcome for all parties.

Following on from that, if Zaha wants a London club that offers a more realistic route to the first-team, then QPR could appeal, and they would benefit from the attacker in the squad.

Julien Stephan’s side will also fancy their chances of winning promotion, but there will be concerns if they have the squad and the experience to cope with the demands of the Championship.

So, Zaha would help on both fronts, and he is the sort of unpredictable, skilful player that the R’s fans enjoy watching.

If first-team football is the priority, then Zaha may have to lower his standards, but he would walk into many mid-table Championship sides, including Sheffield United.

Chris Wilder doesn’t have the biggest budget to work with at this level, so he will have his eye on free agents, and he would surely back himself to get Zaha back enjoying his football, which would make him a huge asset at this level.

Swipe on the pitch or use the pad to move.

The Blades are lacking players who have speed and can carry the ball, which is where Zaha excels, so he is the profile that could enhance the Yorkshire outfit as they seek a top eight finish.

Finally, Derby are in a desperate situation right now, so it’s perhaps unrealistic to think that they could attract a player like Zaha.

However, if the ex-Lyon man wants regular football, he knows he would get it at Pride Park, and, at this stage of his career, that may be something that Zaha decides to prioritise.

Derby are going to need to improve John Eustace’s squad when they get the chance, otherwise they will drop to League One. Whilst they must strengthen defensively, a player with pace and the ability to threaten on the counter is also imperative, which is what Zaha is all about.

#2119. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2118. GdS: Amorim hoping for three recoveries during the break to lighten injury crisis

AC Milan head coach Ruben Amorim hopes to welcome three players back during the international break, a report claims.

La Gazzetta dello Sport this morning provided updates on the possible return dates for Strahinja Pavlovic, Fikayo Tomori and Ruben Loftus-Cheek, each of whom missed the win over Genoa due to various muscle issues.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The paper states that Amorim hopes to have all three players back for the match against Frosinone on November 22nd, or during the following week. The break will allow the coach to lighten the work load after a particularly intense period.

The players not called up by their respective national teams will take a few days off and return to training on Monday 16 November. The injured players will continue to use Milanello for their recovery.

Pavlovic will not answer the Serbia call-up and will remain in Italy to overcome the injury that prevented him from playing against Ferencváros and Genoa. The central defender will thus avoid further travel and international commitments, allowing him to focus fully on his recovery.

Tomori and Loftus-Cheek are also hopeful. The defender had been sidelined by a right thigh flexor injury, while the midfielder had suffered a left thigh flexor injury. Both will need to be reassessed before being given the all-clear.

As mentioned, the goal is to have them available for the game against high-flying Frosinone, or for the subsequent matches against Olympiacos and Cagliari.

#2117. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”
